- **high-load-triage**: Find the one saturated resource on a slow Linux host: "load average is 40 but CPU is idle", "server is sluggish and I do not know why", "is it CPU, memory or disk". Run a fixed 60-second sequence and write down each reading: uptime, dmesg -T, vmstat 1, mpstat -P ALL 1, pidstat 1, iostat -xz 1, free -m, sar -n DEV 1, sar -n TCP,ETCP 1. Linux load counts uninterruptible D-state tasks, so high load with idle CPU means blocked I/O or a hung mount, not compute. Apply the USE method (utilisation, saturation, errors) per resource, read /proc/pressure for stall time, and check steal on VMs and cgroup… ([greenblacked/AI](https://github.com/greenblacked/AI/tree/HEAD/plugins/linux/skills/high-load-triage))

- **hill-climb**: Runs a global hill-climb optimization loop where the parent is always the current best candidate and the val significance gate decides acceptance. Use as the algorithm for most runs — the first run on a new project, binary pass/fail scorers, and small task sets. Pick how each iteration's reflection is focused with --focus all (every failing val task), cyclic (one task at a time), or hardest-first (lowest-scoring first). Switch to gepa when rollouts are expensive and per-task feedback is rich, or skillopt when you want an annealed edit budget.
